(A.201)

Similarly,

T_{10}(t)=T_{13}(t)+T_{14}(t),

(A.202)

where,

T_{13}(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[-(\partial_{k}^{2}e^{ikx})e^{-itk^{2}}\mathbf{F}\psi(k)-(\partial_{k}^{2}e^{-ikx})\,e^{-itk^{2}}\,S(-k)\mathbf{F}\psi(k)\right]\,(I-g(k^{2}))\,dk,

(A.203)

and

\begin{array}[c]{l}T_{14}(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[e^{ikx}x^{2}(m(k,x)-1)e^{-itk^{2}}\mathbf{F}\psi(k)\right.\\
\\
\left.+e^{-ikx}\,e^{-itk^{2}}x^{2}(m(-k,x)-1)\,S(-k)\mathbf{F}\psi(k)\right]\,(I-g(k^{2})dk.\end{array}

(A.204)

Integrating by parts in (
A.203
) and using (
A.18
),
(
A.22
), (
A.62
), (
A.63
),
(
A.84
), (
A.85
), (
A.86
), (
A.87
), and Parseval’s
identity, we obtain,

\|T_{13}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C(1+|t|^{2})\,\left(\|\psi\|_{L_{2}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+\|\psi\|_{H^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\right).

(A.205)

Further, by (
A.18
), (
A.22
), and (
A.30
),

\|T_{14}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}.

(A.206)

Hence, by (
A.202
), (
A.205
) and (
A.206
),

\|T_{10}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C(1+|t|^{2})\left(\|\psi\|_{L_{2}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+\|\psi\|_{H^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\right).

(A.207)

Equation (
A.169
) follows from (
A.192
), (
A.201
) and
(
A.207
). Let us now prove item 5. By (
A.118
)
with
P_{c}(H)=I,

\begin{array}[c]{l}\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}(t)\,\psi(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-itk^{2}}\left[(ikm(k,x)+(\partial_{x}m(k,x))e^{ikx}\right.\\
\\
\left.+(-ikm(-k,x)+(\partial_{x}m(-k,x))e^{-ikx}S(-k)\right]\mathbf{F}\psi\left(k\right)dk.\end{array}

(A.208)

Equation (
A.170
) follows from (
A.18
), (
A.30
),
(
A.32
), (
A.83
), (
A.208
) and Parseval’s identity.

We prepare the following lemma

LEMMA A.11

Assume that the potential
V
is self-adjoint,
V\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
that the constant boundary
matrices
A,B
fulfill (
A.4
) and (
A.5
), and that
H
has no negative eigenvalues. We define

w(k):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}w(k):=\mathbf{F}\phi(k),\qquad k\geq 0\\
\\
w(k)=S(k)w(-k),\qquad k\leq 0.\end{array}\right.

(A.209)

Then.

1.

w(k)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[f(k,x)^{\dagger}+S(-k)^{\dagger}f(-k,x)^{\dagger}\right]\phi(x)\,dx,\qquad k\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(A.210)

2.

S(k)w(-k)=w(k),\qquad k\in\mathbb{R}.

(A.211)

3.

We have that
\phi\in H^{1}_{A,B}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
if and only if
w\in L^{2}_{1}({\mathbb{R}}),
and for some
C_{1},C_{2}>0,

C_{1}\|w\|_{L^{2}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq\|\phi\|_{H^{1}_{A,B}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C_{2}\|w\|_{L^{2}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.212)

4.

Further, assume that
V\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).
Then, we have that
\phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
and it satisfies the boundary condition (
A.3
) if and only if
w\in L^{2}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}),
and for some
C_{1},C_{2}>0,

C_{1}\|w\|_{L^{2}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C_{2}\|w\|_{L^{2}_{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.213)

5.

Suppose that
V\in L^{1}_{2+j}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),j=1,2.
Then,
\phi\in L^{2}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
if and only if
w\in H^{j}({\mathbb{R}}),j=1,2.
Moreover,

C_{1}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq\|w\|_{H^{j}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C_{2}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{+})},\qquad j=1,2.

(A.214)

Proof.
Let us prove 1. We define
q(k)
by the right-hand side of (
A.210
),

q(k):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[f(k,x)^{\dagger}+S(-k)^{\dagger}f(-k,x)^{\dagger}\right]\phi(x)\,dx,\qquad k\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(A.215)

By (
A.21
) and (
A.209
),
w(k)=q(k),k\geq 0.
Suppose that
k\leq 0.

Then,

\begin{array}[c]{l}w(k)=S(k)w(-k)=S(k)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[f(-k,x)^{\dagger}+S(k)^{\dagger}f(k,x)^{\dagger}\right]\phi(x)\,dx\\
=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[S(-k)^{\dagger}f(-k,x)^{\dagger}+f(k,x)^{\dagger}\right]\phi(x)\,dx=q(k),\end{array}

where we used (
A.18
). It follows that also
w(k)=q(k),k\leq 0.

This proves (
A.210
). Equation (
A.211
) follows from
(
A.18
) and (
A.210
). We now prove 3. Since
H
has no
negative eigenvalues,
\mathbf{F}
is unitary. If
\phi\in H_{A,B}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
it follows from (
A.18
), (
A.83
) and
(
A.209
) that
w\in L_{1}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
and,

\|w\|_{L_{1}^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq\sqrt{2}\|w\|_{L_{1}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|\phi\|_{H_{A,B}^{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).

(A.216)

Suppose that
w\in L_{1}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}).
Denote by
w_{+}
the restriction
of
w
to
\mathbb{R}^{+}.
Then,
\phi=\mathbf{F}^{\dagger}w_{+}.
Further,

kw_{+}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
and by (
A.24
) and functional
calculus,
\phi\in D[\sqrt{H}]=Q(h)=H_{A,B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
(see
(
A.10
)). Moreover, since
D[\sqrt{H}]=H_{A,B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),

it follows from (
A.24
),

\|\phi\|_{H_{A,B}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\left[\|\sqrt{H}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\right]\leq C\|w_{+}\|_{L_{1}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|w\|_{L_{1}^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.217)

Equation (
A.212
) follows from (
A.216
) and (
A.217
).

Let us prove item 4. Suppose that
\phi\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
and that it
satisfies the boundary condition (
A.3
). Then, by (
A.13
)

\phi\in D[H]
and by (
A.24
),

\|k^{2}w\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}=\|H\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq\|-\phi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+\|V\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}.

(A.218)

Further by (
A.18
), (
A.211
) and (
A.218
)

\|w\|_{L_{2}^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}=\sqrt{2}\|w\|_{L_{2}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|\phi\|_{H^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.219)

Assume that
w\in L_{2}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}).
Further, by (
A.24
)

\phi\in D[H],
and

\|H\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}=\|k^{2}w_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}.

(A.220)

Then, moreover, as
D[H]\subset H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
using (
A.220
)
we have,

\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C[\|H\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}]\leq C\|w_{+}\|_{L_{2}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|w\|_{L_{2}^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.221)

Equation (
A.213
) follows from (
A.218
) and (
A.221
).
This completes the proof of 4. Let us prove 5. By (
A.86
) and
(
A.87
) if
\phi\in L_{j}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),j=1,2
we have that

w\in H^{j}(\mathbb{R}^{+})\cup H^{j}(\mathbb{R}^{-}),j=1,2.
However, by
(
A.210
)
w
and
\partial_{k}w
are continuous at
k=0,
and then,
w\in H^{j}({\mathbb{R}}),j=1,2.
Moreover, using (
A.18
),
(
A.62
), (
A.63
), (
A.86
),
(
A.87
), and (
A.209
),

\|w\|_{H^{j}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|\phi\|_{L_{j}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})},\qquad j=1,2

(A.222)

Suppose that
w\in H^{j}({\mathbb{R}}),

j=1,2.
Since for
k\geq 0,

w_{+}(k)=w(k)=\mathbf{F}\phi,
we have,

\phi(x)=(\mathbf{F}^{\dagger}w_{+})(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[f(k,x)+f(-k,x)S(-k)\right]w(k)\,dk,

(A.223)

and by (
A.18
), and (
A.209
)

\phi(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f(k,x)w(k)\,dk.

(A.224)

By (
A.224
) and as
f(k,x)=e^{ikx}m(k,x),

x^{j}\phi(x)=R_{1,j}(x)+R_{2,j}(x),\qquad j=1,2,

(A.225)

where,

R_{1,j}(x):=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\partial_{k}^{j}e^{ikx})w(k)\,dk,\qquad j=1,2,

(A.226)

and

R_{2,j}(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{ikx}x^{j}(m(k,x)-I)w(k)\,dk,\qquad j=1,2.

(A.227)

Integrating by parts in (
A.226
) and using Parseval’s identity we obtain,

\|R_{1,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|w\|_{H^{j}({\mathbb{R}})},\qquad j=1,2.

(A.228)

Further, by (
A.30
)

\|R_{2,j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|w\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})},\qquad j=1,2.

(A.229)

By (
A.225
), (
A.228
) and (
A.229
)

\|\phi\|_{L_{j}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\|w\|_{H^{j}({\mathbb{R}})},\qquad j=1,2.

(A.230)

and it follows that
\phi\in L_{j}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),j=1,2.
Finally,
(
A.214
) follows from (
A.222
) and (
A.230
).

A.2
The free evolution group

In this subsection we consider the case where the potential
V
is identically zero. Let us denote by
H_{0}
the free Hamiltonian with
potential identically zero,

H_{0}:=H_{A,B,0}.

The free evolution group is given by,

\mathcal{U}_{0}(t):=e^{-itH_{0}}.

Let us denote by
P_{c}(H_{0})
the projector onto the continuous subspace of

H_{0},
that coincides with the projector onto the absolutely continuous
subspace.
As above, we denote by
S_{0}(k)
the scattering matrix
(
A.17
) in the case where the potential is zero, and by

\Psi_{0}(k)
the physical solution for zero potential. It is given by,

\Psi_{0}(k,x)=e^{-ikx}+e^{ikx}S_{0}(k),

(A.231)

where we used that for potential zero the Jost solution is given by
e^{ikx}.

To simplify the notation we denote by
\mathbf{F}_{0}
the generalized Fourier
map (
A.21
) in the plus case and with zero potential. It is given by,

(\mathbf{F}_{0}Y)(k):=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\,\left(\Psi_{0}(-k,x)\right)^{\dagger}\,Y(x)\,dx,

(A.232)

and the adjoint is given by,

(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\dagger}Y)(x):=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi}}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\,\ \Psi_{0}(-k,x)\,Y(k)\,dk.

(A.233)

The case where
V
is zero (
A.119
) reads

\begin{array}[c]{l}\mathcal{U}_{0}(t)\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\dagger}\phi=\mathcal{U}_{0}(t)\,P_{\operatorname*{c}}(H_{0})\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\dagger}\phi(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{ikx}\,e^{-itk^{2}}\phi\left(k\right)dk\\
\\
+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\,e^{-ikx}e^{-itk^{2}}\,S_{0}(-k)\,\phi\left(k\right)\,dk\\
\\
=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\,e^{ikx}\,e^{-itk^{2}}\,(\mathcal{E}_{0}\phi)(k)\,dk,\end{array}

(A.234)

where we define

\mathcal{E}_{0}\phi(k)=\left\{\begin{array}[c]{l}\phi(k),\qquad k\geq 0,\\
S_{0}(k)\phi(-k),\qquad k<0.\end{array}\right.

(A.235)

Equation (
A.121
) is now given by,

\|\mathcal{E}_{0}\phi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}=\sqrt{2}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})},\qquad\phi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).

(A.236)

Moreover, (
A.122
) reads,

S_{0}(k)(\mathcal{E}_{0}\phi)(-k)=(\mathcal{E}_{0}\phi)(k),\qquad k\in{\mathbb{R}},\phi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).

(A.237)

We denote

\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\phi=\sqrt{\frac{it}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}\phi\left(k\right)dk,

(A.238)

and

\mathcal{Q}_{0}(t):=\mathcal{V}(t)\mathcal{E}_{0}.

(A.239)

Equation (
A.129
) now reads

\mathcal{U}_{0}\left(t\right)\mathbf{F_{0}}^{\dagger}\phi=M\mathcal{D}_{t}\mathcal{Q}_{0}\left(t\right)\phi,\qquad\phi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).

(A.240)

Further, (
A.130
) is now given by,

\mathcal{U}_{0}\left(t\right)P_{c}(H_{0})\psi=M\mathcal{D}_{t}\mathcal{Q}_{0}\left(t\right)\mathbf{F}_{0}\psi,\qquad\psi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).

(A.241)

Moreover, if
H_{0}
has no bound states,
P_{c}(H_{0})=I,
and (
A.131
)
reads now,

\left\|\mathcal{Q}_{0}\left(t\right)\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}=\left\|\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})},

(A.242)

and (
A.132
) is given by,

\mathcal{Q}_{0}^{-1}\left(t\right)\phi=\mathbf{F}_{0}\,e^{itH_{0}}\,M\mathcal{D}_{t}\phi=e^{itk^{2}}\mathbf{F}_{0}M\mathcal{D}_{t}\phi.

(A.243)

Moreover, for
V
equal zero (
A.133
) reads,

\mathbf{F}_{0}e^{itH_{0}}=\mathcal{Q}_{0}^{-1}\,\mathcal{D}_{t}^{-1}\overline{M},

(A.244)

and (
A.134
) corresponds to,

\left\|\mathcal{Q}_{0}^{-1}\left(t\right)\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}=\left\|\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}.

(A.245)

Further (
A.135
) reads,

\mathcal{Q}_{0}^{-1}\left(t\right)\phi(k)=S\left(k\right)\mathcal{V}_{+}\left(t\right)\phi+\mathcal{V}_{-}\left(t\right)\phi,\qquad k\geq 0,

(A.246)

where

\mathcal{V}_{\pm}\left(t\right)\phi(k)=\sqrt{\frac{t}{2\pi i}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{it\left(k\pm\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}\phi\left(x\right)dx,\qquad k\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(A.247)

As in the case of nontrivial potential, for convenience, we have defined the
quantities
\mathcal{V}_{\pm}(t)
for
k\in{\mathbb{R}},
but in
(
A.246
) we only use then for
k\geq 0.
We also find it useful to
introduce the following quantity,

\widehat{\mathcal{V}}(t)\phi(k):=S_{0}\left(k\right)\mathcal{V}^{+}\left(t\right)\phi+\mathcal{V}^{-}\left(t\right)\phi,\qquad k\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(A.248)

Observe that,

\widehat{\mathcal{V}}(t)\phi(k)=\mathcal{Q}_{0}^{-1}\left(t\right)\phi(k),\qquad k\geq 0.

(A.249)

Furthermore, by (
A.18
)

S_{0}(k)\widehat{\mathcal{V}}(t)\phi(-k)=\widehat{\mathcal{V}}(t)\phi(k),\qquad k\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(A.250)

Since for
\phi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),

\sqrt{\frac{it}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{it}{2}\left(k-x\right)^{2}}\phi\left(k\right)dk=\mathcal{F}e^{\frac{ix^{2}}{2t}}\mathcal{F}^{-1}\phi,

(A.251)

it follows from (
A.247
)

\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\phi(x)=\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\mathcal{F}^{-1}M\mathcal{F}\phi\right)\left(\frac{x}{2}\right).

(A.252)

Then, by Parseval’s identity, we see that

\left\|\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\left\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}M\mathcal{F}\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\left\|\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

(A.253)

Moreover,

\begin{array}[c]{l}\left\|\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\phi(x)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\phi(x/2)\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\left\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(M-1\right)\mathcal{F}\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\\
\leq C\left\|\left(\frac{x^{2}}{4t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{F}\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C|t|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\partial_{k}\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})},\end{array}

(A.254)

and similarly,

\left\|\partial_{x}\left(\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\phi(x)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\phi(x/2)\right)\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\left\|\partial_{k}\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

(A.255)

Then, using that

\left\|f\right\|_{{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\partial_{x}f\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{\frac{1}{2}},

we obtain the estimate

\left\|\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\phi(x)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\phi(x/2)\right\|_{{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C|t|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\left\|\partial_{k}\phi\right\|_{{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

(A.256)

Moreover, by (
A.145
) with
m^{\dagger}(k,x)=I,
and Parseval’s identity,
we obtain,

\|\mathcal{V}_{\pm}\phi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}.

(A.257)

Further, by (
A.18
), and (
A.257
),

\|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}\phi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}.

(A.258)

Furthermore, as

\pm\partial_{x}e^{it(k\pm x/2)^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{k}e^{it(k\pm x/2)^{2}},

integrating by parts in (
A.238
) and using (
A.253
) we get,

\left\|\partial_{x}^{n}\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{n}({\mathbb{R}})},\text{ n}\in\mathbb{N\cup}\{0\}\text{.}

(A.259)

Suppose that
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
and that besides being in

L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),\partial_{k}\phi
admits the following decomposition,

\partial_{k}\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},
where
\phi_{1}\in L^{q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
and
\phi_{2}\in L^{q_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
for some

q_{1},q_{2},
with
1\leq q_{1},q_{2}<\infty.
Then, as in the proof of
(
A.150
), but with
m(k,x)=I,
we obtain,

\|\mathcal{V}_{\pm}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\left(\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p_{1}}}\left\|\phi_{1}\right\|_{L^{q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p_{2}}}\left\|\phi_{2}\right\|_{L^{q_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\right),

(A.260)

where
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=1,
and
\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}=1,
and by (
A.18
), (
A.248
) and (
A.260
) we
get,

\|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\left(\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p_{1}}}\left\|\phi_{1}\right\|_{L^{q_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p_{2}}}\left\|\phi_{2}\right\|_{L^{q_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\right),

(A.261)

with
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=1,
and
\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}=1.
Assume that
\phi\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}),
and that besides being in

L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}),\partial_{k}\phi
admits the following decomposition,

\partial_{k}\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},
where
\phi_{1}\in L^{q_{1}}({\mathbb{R}}),
and
\phi_{2}\in L^{q_{2}}({\mathbb{R}}),
for some

q_{1},q_{2},
with
1\leq q_{1},q_{2}<\infty.
Then, by (
A.238
), as
in the proof of (
A.152
), we prove,

\|\mathcal{V}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+}}\leq C\left(\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p_{1}}}\left\|\phi_{1}\right\|_{L^{q_{1}}({\mathbb{R}})}+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p_{2}}}\left\|\phi_{2}\right\|_{L^{q_{2}}({\mathbb{R}})}\right),

(A.262)

where
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=1,
and
\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}=1.

Lemma 1.1

The following estimates hold,

\left\|x\mathcal{V}(t)\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\left(\frac{1}{|t|}\|\partial_{k}\phi(k)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\|k\phi(k)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right),

(A.263)

and

\begin{array}[c]{l}\left\|x^{2}\mathcal{V}(t)\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\left(\frac{1}{|t|}\|k\partial_{k}\phi(k)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\frac{1}{|t|^{2}}\|\partial_{k}^{2}\phi(k)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\frac{1}{|t|}\|\phi(k)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\|k^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right).\end{array}

(A.264)

Proof.
The lemma follows using that
xe^{\pm ikx}=\pm 1/it\,\partial_{k}e^{\pm ikx}
and integrating by parts.

A.3
Asymptotic estimates

We prepare the following lemma

LEMMA A.12

Suppose that
V\in L^{1}_{5/2+\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
for some
\delta>0.
Then

1.

For each
a>0
there is a constant
C_{a}>0
such that

\left\|\left(\mathcal{W}\left(t\right)-\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\right)\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq\frac{C_{a}}{\sqrt{|t|}}\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}})},\qquad|t|\geq a.

(A.265)

2.

\left\|\mathcal{W}\left(t\right)\phi-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}m\left(\frac{x}{2},tx\right)\phi\left(\frac{x}{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq\frac{C}{|t|^{1/4}}\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.266)

.

Proof.
In order to prove (
A.265
) we write

\left(\mathcal{W}\left(t\right)-\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\right)\phi=\sqrt{\frac{it}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}\left(m\left(k,tx\right)-1\right)\phi\left(k\right)dk.

(A.267)

Using that

e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}=\frac{\partial_{k}\left(\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}\right)}{1-2it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}

(A.268)

and integrating by parts we get

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}g(k)dk

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}}{1-2it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}\partial_{k}g(k)dk

\displaystyle-2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{2it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}}{\left(1-2it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}\right)^{2}}g(k)dk.

(A.269)

Then, taking
g=\left(m\left(k,tx\right)-1\right)\phi\left(k\right),
by (
A.30
), (
A.31
) with
\delta>1/2,
via Sobolev’s inequality
theorem we get

\begin{array}[c]{l}\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}\left(m\left(k,tx\right)-1\right)\phi\left(k\right)dk\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\left\|\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\left\|\frac{k}{1+|t|k^{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}})}\\
\\
+C\left\|\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-1-\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\left\|\frac{1}{1+|t|k^{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{a}}{|t|}\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}})},\qquad|t|\geq a.\end{array}

(A.270)

Hence, (
A.265
) follows from (
A.267
), (
A.270
).

Using that

\sqrt{\frac{it}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-itk^{2}}dk=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},

we write

\mathcal{W}\left(t\right)\phi(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}m\left(\frac{x}{2},tx\right)\phi\left(\frac{x}{2}\right)+\sqrt{\frac{it}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}g_{1}dk,

(A.271)

where we denote
g_{1}(k,x)=\left(m\left(k,tx\right)\phi\left(k\right)-m\left(\frac{x}{2},tx\right)\phi\left(\frac{x}{2}\right)\right).
Note that,

\left|g_{1}(k,x)\right|\leq\left|\int_{x/2}^{k}\partial_{s}g_{1}(s,x)ds\right|\leq\sqrt{|k-x/2|}\,\|\partial_{k}g_{1}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.272)

By (
A.269
) with
g=g_{1}
and (
A.272
) we show

\begin{array}[c]{l}\left\|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}g_{1}(\cdot,x)dk\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq C\left\|\frac{k}{1+|t|k^{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\,\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\|\partial_{k}g_{1}(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\\
\\
+C\left\|\frac{\sqrt{k}}{1+|t|k^{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left\|\partial_{k}g_{1}(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq\frac{C}{|t|^{3/4}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left\|\partial_{k}g_{1}(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\end{array}

(A.273)

Then, from (
A.30
), (
A.31
) and (
A.273
) we deduce,

\left\|\sqrt{\frac{it}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}g_{1}dk\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq\frac{C}{|t|^{1/4}}\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}({\mathbb{R}})}.

Together with (
A.271
), this proves (
A.266
).

We prepare the following lemma.

LEMMA A.13

Assume that
V\in L^{1}_{2+\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),
for some
\delta\geq 0.

1.

We have,

\left|\left(\left(\mathcal{W}\left(t\right)-\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\right)\phi\right)\left(x\right)\right|\leq C\sqrt{|t|}\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-1-\delta}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(A.274)

2.

Assume that
\phi\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}),
and that besides being in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}),\partial_{k}\phi
admits the following decomposition,
\partial_{k}\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{2},
where
\phi_{1}\in L^{q_{1}}({\mathbb{R}}),
and
\phi_{2}\in L^{q_{2}}({\mathbb{R}}),
for some
q_{1},q_{2},
with
1\leq q_{1},q_{2}<\infty.
Then, for
1\leq l<\infty,
we have,

\begin{array}[]{l}\left|\left(\mathcal{W}\left(t\right)-\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\right)\phi\left(x\right)\right|\leq C\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-1-\delta}\left(\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p}}\left\|\left\langle k\right\rangle^{-1}\phi_{1}\right\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}})}\right.\\
\\
\left.+|t|^{-\frac{1}{2p_{1}}}\left\|\left\langle k\right\rangle^{-1}\phi_{2}\right\|_{L^{q_{1}}({\mathbb{R}})}\right)+C_{l}|t|^{-\frac{1}{2l}}\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-\delta}\,\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})},\end{array}

(A.275)

with
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=1,
and
\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}=1
, and where
C_{l}
depends on
l.

3.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and

assume that
V\in L^{1}_{2+\delta}\left(\mathbb{R}^{+}\right),
for some
\delta>n.
Then

\left\|\left(\mathcal{W}_{\pm}\left(t\right)-\mathcal{V}_{\pm}\left(t\right)\right)x^{n}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C|t|^{-n}\min\{|t|^{-1/2}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})},\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\},

(A.276)

and

\left\|\left(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}\left(t\right)-\widehat{\mathcal{V}}\left(t\right)\right)x^{n}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C|t|^{-n}\min\{|t|^{-1/2}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})},\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\}.

(A.277)

Proof.
By (
A.126
) and (
A.238
),

\left(\mathcal{W}\left(t\right)-\mathcal{V}\left(t\right)\right)\phi(x)=\sqrt{\frac{it}{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-it\left(k-\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}[m\left(k,tx\right)-I]\phi\left(k\right)dk.

(A.278)

Then (
A.274
), follows from (
A.30
) and (
A.278
). Further,
using (
A.30
), (
A.31
), (
A.269
) with
g=(m(k,tx)-I)\phi(k),
and (
A.278
) we show (
A.275
). Moreover, by (
A.136
) and
(
A.247
)

\left[\left(\mathcal{W}_{\pm}-\mathcal{V}_{\pm}\right)x^{n}\phi\right](k)=\sqrt{\frac{t}{2\pi i}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{it\left(k\pm\frac{x}{2}\right)^{2}}[m^{\dagger}\left(\mp k,tx\right)-I]x^{n}\phi\left(x\right)dx,\qquad k\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(A.279)

Using (
A.30
) we estimate,

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathcal{W}_{\pm}\left(t\right)-\mathcal{V}_{\left(\pm\right)}\left(t\right)\right)x^{n}\phi\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{t}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-1-\delta}x^{n}\left|\phi\left(x\right)\right|dx

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{t}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{-1}\min\{\left\|\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-1-\delta}x^{n}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})},\left\|\left\langle tx\right\rangle^{-1-\delta}x^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\left\|\phi\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\}.

(A.280)

Equation (
A.276
) follows from (
A.280
). Estimate (
A.277
)
follows from (
A.18
) and (
A.276
).
